Source: https://motion.dev/docs/react-animate-suspense

# AnimateSuspense

Motion+ (paid). Not included in the free motion package. Install Motion UI: https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation. Import: `import { AnimateSuspense } from "motion-plus/animate-suspense"`. Animate React Suspense fallbacks in and out, including server-rendered fallbacks replaced by streamed content.

`AnimateSuspense` is an animated version of React's [`Suspense`](https://react.dev/reference/react/Suspense) component. It allows you to animate a loading fallback out when the content it waits for is ready.

React's `Suspense` removes its fallback the moment content resolves. `AnimateSuspense` keeps it in the DOM until its `exit` animation has finished, the same way [`AnimatePresence`](https://motion.dev/docs/react-animate-presence) does for removed elements.

```
<AnimateSuspense
  fallback={
    <motion.div
      initial={{ opacity: 0 }}
      animate={{ opacity: 1 }}
      exit={{ opacity: 0 }}
    >
      Loading...
    </motion.div>
  }
>
  <Comments />
</AnimateSuspense>
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/animate-suspense-pop-layout

It also works with server rendering. A fallback that streams from the server is handed over to the client on hydration, so it can still animate out when the streamed content arrives.

`AnimateSuspense` is exclusive to [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) members. Motion+ is a one-time payment, lifetime membership that unlocks Motion UI, premium examples and access to a private Discord community.

## Install

First, [install Motion UI](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation) in your project. You need to be a [Motion+ member](https://motion.dev/plus) to generate an access token.

Once installed, `AnimateSuspense` can be imported via `motion-plus/animate-suspense`.

```
import { AnimateSuspense } from "motion-plus/animate-suspense"
```

`AnimateSuspense` requires `motion-plus@4.3.0`, `motion@14.1.0` and React 18 or above.

## Usage

`AnimateSuspense` shares the same API as `Suspense`. Replace `Suspense` with `AnimateSuspense`, and give the `fallback` an `exit` animation.

```
<AnimateSuspense
  fallback={<motion.div exit={{ opacity: 0 }}>Loading...</motion.div>}
>
  <Comments />
</AnimateSuspense>
```

When `Comments` suspends, for instance while it loads data with [`use`](https://react.dev/reference/react/use) or a [`lazy`](https://react.dev/reference/react/lazy) component loads, the fallback renders. When it resolves, the content renders and the fallback plays its `exit` animation before it's removed.

If the content doesn't suspend, the fallback never mounts, not even for a single frame.

The fallback doesn't need a `key`. `AnimateSuspense` adds one for you.

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/react-animate-suspense


---

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/react-carousel

# Carousel

Motion+ (paid). Not included in the free motion package. Install Motion UI: https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation. Import: `import { Carousel } from "motion-plus/react"`. A performant, accessible, and infinitely scrollable carousel for React.

The Carousel component creates performant, accessible and fully-featured carousels in React. It's designed to be flexible and easy to use, supporting pointer, wheel and keyboard navigation out the box.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/carousel

It allows you to go beyond the traditional limitations of CSS-only approaches, with support for infinitely-scrolling carousels and without limitations on styling.

### Features

- **Lightweight:** Just `+5.5kb` on top of [the ](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-component)`motion`[ component](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-component).

- **Accessible:** Automatic ARIA labels, respects reduced motion, RTL layouts, and all major input methods.

- **Performant:** Built on the same [unique rendering](https://motion.dev/magazine/building-the-ultimate-ticker) used by the [Ticker](https://motion.dev/docs/react-ticker) component that achieves infinite scrolling with while minimising or eliminating item cloning.

- **Customisable:** Provides functions and state to easily create custom controls and pagination.

## Install

First, [install Motion UI](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation) in your project. You need to be a [Motion+ member](https://motion.dev/plus) to generate an access token.

## Usage

### Import

Import the `Carousel` component from "motion-plus/react"\`:

```
import { Carousel } from "motion-plus/react"
```

`Carousel` accepts a single mandatory prop, `items`. This is a list of valid React nodes (which can be components, strings or numbers):

```
const items = [
  <span>One</span>,
  <span>Two</span>,
  <span>Three</span>
]

return <Carousel items={items} />
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/carousel-coverflow

### Direction

By default, carousels will scroll horizontally. Setting the `axis` prop to `y`, we can make them vertical.

```
<Carousel items={items} axis="y" />
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/carousel-vertical

### Layout

Items are laid out via flexbox. Passing `gap` and `align` will adjust the spacing and off-axis alignment of them items.

```
<Carousel items={items} gap={0} align="start" />
```

### Overflow

By setting `overflow` to `true`, items will visually extend out from the container to the edges of the viewport.

```
<Carousel items={items} overflow />
```

This makes it straightforward to place a `Carousel` within a document flow but still extend the ticker effect across the full viewport.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/carousel-pagination-arrows

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/react-carousel

# Cursor

Motion+ (paid). Not included in the free motion package. Install Motion UI: https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation. Import: `import { Cursor } from "motion-plus/react"`. Custom cursor and follow-along effects for React.

`Cursor` is a powerful React component for building creative and interactive cursor effects. Effortlessly replace the default browser cursor, create engaging follow-cursor animations, or add magnetic snapping to UI elements.

Built on Motion's [layout animations](https://motion.dev/docs/react-layout-animations), `Cursor` is performant and full customisable with variants, CSS and custom React components.

```
<Cursor />
```

`Cursor` is exclusive to [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) members. Motion+ is a one-time payment, lifetime membership that unlocks Motion UI, premium examples and access to a private Discord community.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/ios-pointer

## Features

- **Two modes: **Easily switch between replacing the default cursor or creating a "follow" cursor effect.

- **State-aware:** Automatically adapts its appearance when hovering over links, buttons, or selectable text, and when pressed.

- **Magnetic:** Make the cursor snap to interactive elements on hover for a tactile feel.

- **Customisable: **Use CSS, Motion variants, and custom React components to create any cursor you can imagine.

- **Accessible:** Can be disabled for users who prefer reduced motion.

## Install

First, [install Motion UI](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation) in your project. You need to be a [Motion+ member](https://motion.dev/plus) to generate an access token.

## Usage

The `Cursor` component is used for both custom cursor and follow cursor effects:

```
import { Cursor } from "motion-plus/react"
```

When `Cursor` is rendered, a default custom cursor will render on the page, hiding the browser's default cursor.

```
<Cursor />
```

You can remove the cursor and restore the browser cursor at any time by removing the component.

```
{isCursorVisible ? <Cursor /> : null}
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/cursor

### Styling

By default, the cursor is a neutral grey color. It's possible to change the cursor's styles using CSS.

```
<Cursor className="my-cursor" style={{ backgroundColor: "red" }} />
```

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/cursor

# Gallery

Motion+ (paid). Not included in the free motion package. Install Motion UI: https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation. Import: `import { Gallery } from "motion-plus/gallery"`. An image and video gallery for React, with 2D and 3D arrangements, gestures and selection that all transition smoothly into each other.

`Gallery` is a React component for creating dynamic image and video galleries in 2D and 3D.

Lay items out as a list, grid, barrel, coverflow, helix, ring, sphere or card stack, then drag, scroll or key through them.

Change the arrangement, open an item or add and remove items, and every change animates smoothly from wherever the gallery is.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-playground

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={barrel()} aria-label="Collection">
  <Gallery.Image src="/a.jpg" alt="Runner" width={1200} height={1600} />
  <Gallery.Video src="/b.mp4" poster="/b.jpg" alt="Shoe rotating" />
</Gallery>
```

### Features

- **Eight arrangements:** `list`, `grid`, `barrel`, `coverflow`, `helix`, `ring`, `sphere` and `stack`, or write your own with `defineGalleryArrangement`.

- **Smooth everything:** Arrangement swaps, opening an item, adding and removing items all blend from the current pose, with springs and stagger.

- **Inputs:** Drag with momentum, wheel and keyboard navigation out the box, with snapping and infinite looping.

- **Detail views:** Open an item into its own box with a thumbnail strip, or reveal a panel around it. Higher-res assets are loaded automatically.

- **Velocity effects:** `tilt` and `ripple` modifiers link items to the gallery's speed.

- **Shaders:** Draw items with WebGPU through shaders like `depthOfField` and `reflection`. Without WebGPU, the DOM draws them as normal.

- **Accessible:** Uses the correct ARIA tags, respects reduced motion and supports keyboard navigation.

- **Performant:** All items animated with `transform`, and only visible media loads and plays.

## Install

First, [install Motion UI](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation) in your project. You need to be a [Motion+ member](https://motion.dev/plus) to generate an access token.

## Usage

### Import

Import `Gallery` from `"motion-plus/gallery"`:

```jsx
import { Gallery } from "motion-plus/gallery"
```

Arrangements, modifiers and presence presets are separate imports, so only the ones you use are bundled:

```jsx
import { Gallery, barrel, tilt } from "motion-plus/gallery"
```

### Items

`Gallery` accepts three kinds of item as children.

- `Gallery.Image` renders an `img`. `alt` is required. Use `""` for decorative images.

- `Gallery.Video` renders a `video`. `alt` is required and is applied as `aria-label`.

- `Gallery.Item` renders an `li` with any content inside.

```jsx
<Gallery aria-label="Studio work">
  <Gallery.Image
    src="/photo.jpg"
    alt="Dancer mid-turn"
    width={800}
    height={1000}
  />
  <Gallery.Video src="/clip.mp4" poster="/clip.jpg" alt="Product turntable" />
  <Gallery.Item>
    <h2>Any content</h2>
  </Gallery.Item>
</Gallery>
```

Each item can take a `value`. This is its identity for `active`, `selected`, callbacks and hooks. Without one, items are identified by their index.

```jsx
<Gallery.Image value="runner" src="/runner.jpg" alt="Runner" />
```

### Size

Give the gallery a size with CSS, and its items a size with the `--gallery-item-width` and `--gallery-item-height` CSS variables. Items default to `240px` by `320px`.

```css
.gallery {
  height: 100vh;
}

.gallery [data-gallery-item] {
  --gallery-item-width: 300px;
  --gallery-item-height: 400px;
}
```

The engine owns each item's position, size, transform and opacity. Everything else, like `border-radius`, `background` and `filter`, is yours to style.

### Arrangements

Pass an arrangement to `arrange`. The default is `list()`.

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={helix({ itemsPerTurn: 8 })} />
```

Change `arrange` at any time and the gallery animates into the new shape.

#### `list`

Items along a straight track: horizontal, vertical (`axis="y"`) or diagonal (`angle`).

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-list

| Option   | Default | Description                                                                  |
| -------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `angle`  | `0`     | Direction of the track in degrees. `-30` gives a rising diagonal.            |
| `curl`   | `0`     | Degrees of `rotateY` per item away from focus.                               |
| `depth`  | `0`     | Pixels pushed back per item away from focus, either side.                    |
| `recede` | `0`     | Pixels pushed back per item along the track, like a staircase into the page. |
| `turn`   | `0`     | `rotateY` in degrees for every item.                                         |
| `wave`   | `0`     | Amplitude in pixels of a sine wave across the track.                         |
| `room`   | `24`    | Pixels kept clear around an item opened inline.                              |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={list({ angle: -30, curl: 20, depth: 40, wave: 30 })} />
```

#### `grid`

An infinite, pannable plane. When looping, which is the default, it's tiled with clones so its edge is never visible.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-grid

| Option    | Default      | Description                                                                                           |
| --------- | ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `columns` | `"auto"`     | Columns in the repeating block. `"auto"` fits the viewport.                                           |
| `fill`    | `"sequence"` | How the plane repeats when the item count doesn't fill a block: `"sequence"`, `"offset"` or `"none"`. |
| `rotateX` | `0`          | Leans the plane back, in degrees.                                                                     |
| `rotateY` | `0`          | Brings the plane's left side forward, in degrees.                                                     |
| `rotateZ` | `0`          | Turns the plane clockwise, in degrees.                                                                |
| `bulge`   | `0`          | Pixels the plane bulges towards the viewer at focus.                                                  |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={grid({ columns: 6, fill: "offset", rotateX: 20, bulge: 120 })} />
```

#### `barrel`

Items around a cylinder. `axis="y"` gives a vertical barrel. A barrel always loops.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-barrel

| Option    | Default  | Description                                                                |
| --------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `radius`  | `"auto"` | Cylinder radius in pixels. `"auto"` spaces items by their size plus `gap`. |
| `rotateX` | `-8`     | Camera tilt in degrees. Negative views the barrel from above.              |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={barrel({ radius: 600, rotateX: -12 })} axis="y" />
```

#### `coverflow`

The classic: a flat focused item with angled, overlapping neighbours. Coverflow spaces items with its own options, so it ignores `gap`.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-coverflow

| Option          | Default | Description                                                                                                  |
| --------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `turn`          | `55`    | `rotateY` of side items, in degrees.                                                                         |
| `spread`        | `0.3`   | Spacing between side items, as a fraction of item width.                                                     |
| `depth`         | `0.5`   | How far side items sit behind the focused one, as a fraction of width.                                       |
| `centerSpacing` | `0.8`   | Spacing between the focused item and each neighbour, as a fraction of width. From `1.05` it's a plain slide. |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={coverflow({ turn: 45, spread: 0.25, depth: 0.6, centerSpacing: 0.9 })} />
```

#### `helix`

A spiral. The default `axis` is `"y"`, which gives a vertical spiral. `axis="x"` gives a horizontal corkscrew.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-helix

| Option         | Default  | Description                                                                |
| -------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `radius`       | `"auto"` | Radius in pixels. `"auto"` spaces a turn's items by their size plus `gap`. |
| `itemsPerTurn` | `10`     | Items per full turn.                                                       |
| `pitch`        | `"auto"` | Pixels travelled along the axis per item. `"auto"` is 22% of an item.      |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={helix({ radius: 500, itemsPerTurn: 12, pitch: 48 })} />
```

#### `ring`

Items around a flat circle, like a dial. An `arc` under `360` fans them out.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-ring

| Option    | Default  | Description                                                    |
| --------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `radius`  | `"auto"` | Circle radius in pixels.                                       |
| `arc`     | `360`    | Degrees of arc to spread items over. A full ring always loops. |
| `upright` | `false`  | Keep items upright instead of rotating them around the circle. |
| `flip`    | `false`  | Curve towards the other side of the track.                     |
| `rotateX` | `0`      | Camera tilt in degrees.                                        |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={ring({ arc: 120, upright: true, flip: true, rotateX: 10 })} />
```

#### `sphere`

Items on a sphere. Dragging spins it both ways. A sphere always loops.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-sphere

| Option   | Default             | Description                                                                          |
| -------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `radius` | `"auto"`            | Radius in pixels. `"auto"` is the smallest sphere where neighbours stay `gap` apart. |
| `gap`    | The gallery's `gap` | Pixels between neighbouring items.                                                   |
| `face`   | `"camera"`          | `"camera"` keeps items facing the viewer. `"outward"` lies them flat on the surface. |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={sphere({ radius: 700, gap: 12, face: "outward" })} />
```

#### `stack`

A deck of cards. Swipe the top card away to reveal the next. A stack spaces items with its own options, so it ignores `gap`.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/gallery-stack

| Option    | Default | Description                                             |
| --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `peek`    | `14`    | Pixels each card behind the top one peeks out above it. |
| `depth`   | `50`    | Pixels each card behind the top one is pushed back.     |
| `visible` | `4`     | Number of cards visible behind the top card.            |

```jsx
<Gallery arrange={stack({ peek: 20, depth: 60, visible: 3 })} />
```

Every numeric arrangement option, apart from `grid`'s `columns`, also accepts a motion value, so it can be animated or linked to scroll without a re-render.

```jsx
const rotateX = useMotionValue(-8)

return <Gallery arrange={barrel({ rotateX })} />
```

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/react-gallery


---

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/react-scramble-text

# ScrambleText

Motion+ (paid). Not included in the free motion package. Install Motion UI: https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation. Import: `import { ScrambleText } from "motion-plus/react"`. A scramble text animation component for React, with stagger and renderless control.

`ScrambleText` is a 1kb React component for creating scramble text animations.

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/scramble-text-stagger-center

Unlike most scramble text implementations, `ScrambleText` uses Motion for React's [motion value](https://motion.dev/docs/motion-value) rendering to avoid React re-renders.

```
<ScrambleText>{text}</ScrambleText>
```

`ScrambleText` is exclusive to [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) members. Motion+ is a one-time payment, lifetime membership that unlocks Motion UI, premium examples and access to a private Discord community.

## Features

- **Stagger:** Use Motion's `stagger` function for both `delay` and `duration` to create letter-by-letter reveal effects.

- **Performant:** Renders via Motion's high-performance animation loop, skipping React re-renders.

- **Playback control:** Toggle the `active` prop to start/stop scrambling, perfect for hover effects or scroll-triggered animations.

- **Customisable characters:** Use the default alphanumeric set, or provide custom characters (including emoji).

## Install

First, [install Motion UI](https://motion.dev/docs/react-motion-ui-installation) in your project. You need to be a [Motion+ member](https://motion.dev/plus) to generate an access token.

## Usage

By passing a string as the `ScrambleText` child, it will animate that text in character by character.

```
<ScrambleText>Hello world!</ScrambleText>
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/scramble-text

### Stagger

Use Motion's `stagger()` function to reveal characters one by one:

```
import { stagger } from "motion"
import { ScrambleText } from "motion-plus/react"

// Start scrambling at the same time, reveal after 1
// second, with a 0.05 delay between each character/
<ScrambleText duration={stagger(0.05, { startDelay: 1 })} >
  Hello world!
</ScrambleText>
```

#### Delay

Stagger when each character starts scrambling by passing `stagger` to `delay`:

```
// Chars start scrambling one-by-one, all reveal after 1s of scrambling
<ScrambleText delay={stagger(0.1)} duration={1}>
  Hello world!
</ScrambleText>
```

#### Direction

Use `stagger`'s `from` option for controlling the direction of stagger:

```
<ScrambleText
  delay={stagger(0.05, { from: "center" })}
  duration={0.5}
>
  Hello world!
</ScrambleText>
```

Live example: https://examples.motion.dev/react/scramble-text-stagger-center

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/react-scramble-text
