# Motion Studio

Edit Motion animations directly on your website with a visual editor and fast animation editor AI. Then apply changes to source with your existing code agent.

Motion Studio puts a timeline directly on your website. Select an animation, edit visually with AI, apply direct to source code.

Edit and preview animations without leaving your website. A visual animation editor combined with a fast animation editing agent.

Motion Studio is free to install and edit animations visually. Apply to code needs [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus). The [Ultramotion](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-ultramotion) editing agent needs Motion+ Pro, and every signed-in account gets 5 free Ultramotion prompts each month.

## Supported environments

Studio runs in your local development environment. The bundler integration and the animation code that reaches the browser determine what Studio can edit.

| Environment | Support |
| --- | --- |
| Next.js with Turbopack | Supported with `withMotionStudio` and the companion Studio server. |
| Next.js with Webpack | Supported with the same Next.js wrapper and companion server. |
| Vite | Supported with the `motionStudio()` plugin. |
| Webpack | Supported with the development-only Studio loader and companion server. |
| Nuxt with Vite | Supported with `motionStudio({ serverUrl })` and the companion Studio server. Use explicit Motion imports in Vue components. |
| Astro | Supported with the same Vite plugin and companion server for imported client scripts and hydrated React or Vue components. |

Nuxt and Astro were verified with Nuxt 4.5.2, Astro 7.3.3 and Node.js 22.19.0. Checks cover animation discovery, preview, Apply, hot reload and production builds without Studio code. Your framework may need a newer Node.js version than Studio's minimum.

### React Server Component boundaries

In the Next.js App Router, keep animations you want to edit in client modules. Studio preserves the `"use client"` directive and does not add it to Server Components. The panel starts only in the browser.

A Server Component can render an animated Client Component. Put `"use client"` at the top of the client entry file, before its imports:

```tsx
"use client"

import { motion } from "motion/react"

export function AnimatedCard() {
  return <motion.div animate={{ opacity: 1 }} initial={{ opacity: 0 }} />
}
```

Keep server data fetching in the Server Component and pass serialisable props to the Client Component. Studio discovers animation code that runs in the browser. Animation definitions that run only on the server are outside that discovery path. See the [Next.js guide to Server and Client Components](https://nextjs.org/learn/react-foundations/server-and-client-components) for boundary rules.

### Nuxt and Astro

Nuxt and Astro control their own HTML output. Set `serverUrl` on the Vite plugin so instrumented client modules can load the panel from the companion server. This setup does not depend on Vite's HTML hook.

In Nuxt, import the Motion APIs you use from `motion-v` in each Vue file. Studio needs these imports to discover the animation source. Nuxt auto-imports alone are not enough.

In Astro, put JavaScript animations in a `.js` or `.ts` file imported by a client script. React and Vue components need a `client:*` directive. Studio waits for an island to hydrate before starting a component preview. Server-only components, Astro frontmatter and animation definitions written directly inside `.astro` script blocks are not supported.

## Install

Motion Studio requires **Motion 13.3.0 or later** and **Node.js 22.13 or later**, or the newer Node.js version your framework requires. Studio runs in your local **Vite, Next.js, Nuxt, Astro or Webpack** development environment.

Copy this prompt into your agent to install Motion Studio.

<details className="studio-install-details">
<summary>Show manual setup</summary>

### Install the package

Run this command from your app's directory:

```bash
npm install -D motion-studio
```

Use your project's package manager if it uses Yarn, pnpm or Bun. Then follow the steps for your framework. Keep your existing configuration and add the Studio integration to it.

### Vite

Add `motionStudio()` to the `plugins` array in `vite.config.ts`, before your React or Vue plugin:

```ts
import { motionStudio } from "motion-studio"
import { defineConfig } from "vite"
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react"

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [motionStudio(), react()],
})
```

For Vue, keep your existing `vue()` plugin in place of `react()`. For plain JavaScript, use `plugins: [motionStudio()]`.

Run your normal development command. Vite starts the Studio server for you. The plugin only runs in development.

### Next.js

Wrap your existing config with `withMotionStudio` in `next.config.mjs` or `next.config.ts`:

```ts
import { withMotionStudio } from "motion-studio/next"

const nextConfig = {
  // Keep your existing Next.js options here.
}

export default withMotionStudio(nextConfig)
```

The wrapper supports both Turbopack and Webpack. It enables Studio during `next dev` and leaves production builds unchanged.

Run your normal development command. In a second terminal, start the Studio server from the same app directory:

```bash
npx motion-studio serve
```

Keep both commands running. Studio uses `http://localhost:5200` by default. If you change the server port, pass the matching `serverUrl` as the second argument to `withMotionStudio`.

### Nuxt

Add the plugin to `vite.plugins` in `nuxt.config.ts`. Keep your existing Nuxt options and plugins:

```ts
import { motionStudio } from "motion-studio"

export default defineNuxtConfig({
  vite: {
    plugins: [motionStudio({ serverUrl: "http://localhost:5200" })],
  },
})
```

Run your normal Nuxt development command. In a second terminal, run `npx motion-studio serve` from the same app directory. Keep both commands running. This integration supports Nuxt's Vite builder.

Use explicit imports in your Vue components:

```vue
<script setup>
import { motion } from "motion-v"
</script>

<template>
  <motion.div :animate="{ opacity: 1 }" />
</template>
```

### Astro

Add the plugin to `vite.plugins` in `astro.config.mjs` or `astro.config.ts`. Keep your existing integrations, including any React or Vue integration:

```ts
import { defineConfig } from "astro/config"
import { motionStudio } from "motion-studio"

export default defineConfig({
  vite: {
    plugins: [motionStudio({ serverUrl: "http://localhost:5200" })],
  },
})
```

Run your normal Astro development command. In a second terminal, run `npx motion-studio serve` from the same app directory. Keep both commands running.

For JavaScript animations, create a client module such as `src/scripts/animate.ts`:

```ts
import { animate } from "motion"

animate(".card", { opacity: [0, 1] }, { duration: 0.4 })
```

Import it from the page's client script. For a page in `src/pages/`, this can be:

```astro
<div class="card">Hello</div>
<script>
  import "../scripts/animate"
</script>
```

Keep the animation definition in the imported file so Studio can edit it. For React or Vue animations, hydrate the component, for example with `<AnimatedCard client:load />`. A component rendered only on the server cannot appear in the timeline.

For Nuxt and Astro, the plugin runs only during development. Production builds exclude Studio. If you change the companion server port, set the same port in `serverUrl`.

### Webpack

Add a development-only pre-loader to your existing `module.rules` in `webpack.config.cjs`. Keep your other rules and options:

```js
module.exports = (_env, argv) => ({
  module: {
    rules: [
      ...(argv.mode === "development" ? [{
        test: /\.(?:[cm]?[jt]s|[jt]sx)$/,
        exclude: /node_modules/,
        enforce: "pre",
        use: [{
          loader: require.resolve("motion-studio/loader"),
          options: { boot: { serverUrl: "http://localhost:5200" } },
        }],
      }] : []),
      // Keep your existing rules here.
    ],
  },
})
```

Run your development server with `--mode development`. In a second terminal, run `npx motion-studio serve` from the app directory. Keep both commands running. The Studio loader must be excluded from production builds.

### Open Studio

Open your local app and press the timeline button. Play an animation if the timeline is empty, then preview a timing change. Log in from the Studio panel to use your subscription.

<span id="connect-an-agent" />

### Connect external agent

Choose a provider in your project's `.env.local`:

```ini
MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_PROVIDER=cursor
```

The supported values are `cursor`, `codex` and `claude`. Cursor is the default. Studio reads `.env.local` on each request, so changes take effect without a restart. Restart the dev server if you change its process environment or install an SDK.

**Cursor SDK** is included. Use **Connect Cursor** in the panel to sign in. An existing Cursor SDK sign-in is reused.

**Codex** needs its SDK installed separately:

```bash
npm install -D @openai/codex-sdk
npx codex login
```

**Claude** needs its SDK and Claude Code sign-in:

```bash
npm install -D @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk
claude auth login
```

If the Claude command is missing, install [Claude Code](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup). The editor reuses its sign-in.

Set `MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_MODEL` to override the provider's default model. The selected model must be available to your account.

| Provider | Model when no override is set                                      |
| -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Cursor   | `composer-latest`, which follows Cursor's current Composer model   |
| Codex    | The model selected by Codex configuration, or its built-in default |
| Claude   | The Claude Code SDK default model                                  |

For example, to use the Composer alias explicitly:

```ini
MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_PROVIDER=cursor
MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_MODEL=composer-latest
```

Cursor's local SDK needs a model, so Studio supplies the alias. Studio omits the model option for Codex and Claude unless you set an override. This avoids a Studio release for each new model. Your installed SDK must still support the selected model. Codex runs with low reasoning effort.

Studio checks the process environment first, then `.env.local` and `.env` in the app directory, then those files in its parent directory. The first non-empty value wins. Leave `MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_MODEL` unset to use the defaults above.

You can preview and apply literal edits without an agent. With Motion+ Pro, chat works without an agent too: Ultramotion edits the timeline with your Studio login. Ultramotion has a monthly allowance for each account. When it is used up, the chat asks you to connect a coding agent until the allowance resets. Writing a new animation or an agent edit to source needs a connected agent. Without one, **Apply** copies the prompt to your clipboard so you can paste it into any coding agent, and the chat explains how to connect one.

</details>

## Next steps

- [Editing](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-editing): edit tracks, keyframes and easing on the timeline, then apply to code.
- [Ultramotion](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-ultramotion): edit animations with natural language.
- [Reduced motion](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-reduced-motion): preview and edit what users who ask for reduced motion see.

<span id="supported-animation-forms" />

## Supported animation APIs

Motion Studio currently supports `animate()`, `<motion.*>`, `useAnimate()` and `v-motion`.

It works with all React, JS, Vue, Three.js and vgpu flavours of Motion.

## Troubleshooting

**The panel does not appear.** Confirm the app runs in development. Press the timeline button on the page. For Next.js, Nuxt, Astro and Webpack, check that the editor server is running and that its port matches `serverUrl`. In Nuxt and Astro, load a component or client module with a supported animation so it can start the panel.

**A Nuxt or Astro animation is missing.** In Nuxt, add explicit `motion-v` imports to the Vue file. In Astro, move inline animation code to an imported `.js` or `.ts` file, or use a hydrated React or Vue component. An island appears after it hydrates.

**No animations appear.** Trigger the interaction first. Check that the file imports from `"motion"` rather than `"framer-motion"`.

**Why is my value read-only?** Values set as expressions in your code currently appear as read-only in the timeline.

**Apply reports a stale file.** The source changed since the preview was made. Reload the page and make the edit again. Studio rejects stale edits to protect changes made in your code editor.

**Apply asks me to sign up.** Apply and the agent need Motion+ or Motion+ Pro. Press **Log in** if you already have one. If you were logged in before, your Pro subscription may have ended or the login was revoked; log in again after you renew.

**The agent is unavailable.** Check `MOTION_STUDIO_AGENT_PROVIDER`, install that provider's SDK, and complete its sign-in. Restart the dev server after you install an SDK.

**A custom local hostname cannot save.** Allow the app's origin when you start the companion server:

```bash
npx motion-studio serve --origin https://app.local:3000
```

The page must be able to reach the companion server. Use a local HTTPS proxy if your browser blocks an HTTP server from an HTTPS page.

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/studio-editing

# Editing animations

Edit Motion animations on a visual timeline in your running site, then apply the changes to your source code.

Animations will appear in the timeline editor when detected. For JS, animations are detected when they run whereas many React and Vue animations will be parsed from props.

Select an animation from the dropdown to start editing.

Each group of elements within an animation are represented by a track.

A track can be dragged to move an animation (by setting its delay). It has resize handles at each end which can be used to rescale the animation and the duration of all its keyframes.

Double-click a track to add a keyframe, or select a keyframe to edit its values or easing.

Double click a keyframe to snap it to the nearest keyframe.

Changes stay in the preview until Apply. Use the discard control to remove
pending changes.

Use **Cmd+Z** or **Ctrl+Z** to undo. Add Shift to redo. Draft edits and applied source edits have undo history. Source history belongs to the current dev server session.

## Add a new animation

By pressing the **Add animation** button, you can select an element (or multiple elements) to apply a new animation to.

Choose Animation, Hover, Press, or In view.

To edit what users who ask for reduced motion see, see [Reduced motion](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-reduced-motion).

## Keyboard controls

Use **Cmd** on macOS or **Ctrl** on Windows and Linux. **Option** on macOS is the same as **Alt** below.

### Panel and timeline

These shortcuts work while Studio is open, except when you're typing in a field. **Alt+T** also works when the panel is closed.

| Shortcut | Action |
| --- | --- |
| **Alt+T** | Open or close Studio. |
| **Space** | Play or pause the selected animation. |
| **Space+drag** | Pan the timeline. Release Space without dragging to play or pause. |
| **Left arrow** / **Right arrow** | Move the playhead to the previous or next keyframe. |
| **Home** | Move the playhead to the start. |
| **Cmd/Ctrl+A** | Select all tracks in the current animation. |
| **Cmd/Ctrl+Z** | Undo an edit. |
| **Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Z** | Redo an edit. |
| **Shift+A** | Cycle the Studio accent colour. |

Press **Cmd/Ctrl+S** to apply pending changes for the selected animation. This also works while typing in a field. Other animations keep their pending changes. Apply needs Motion+ or Motion+ Pro.

### Editing and chat

| Shortcut | Action |
| --- | --- |
| **Enter** in the agent input | Send your message. |
| **Shift+Enter** in the agent input | Add a new line. |
| **Arrow keys** with a BÃ©zier control point focused | Move the control point by 0.1. Hold **Shift** for smaller steps of 0.01. |
| **Escape** while picking an element | Cancel element selection. |

Text fields keep their normal text-editing shortcuts, including undo and redo.

## Apply to code

Pressing Apply will write your new animation to your codebase. Simple edits are written straight to code by the Motion Studio server.

With a connected agent, pressing Apply will automatically write the animation to your codebase.

Without one, a prompt will be copied to your clipboard to paste into the agent of your choice. See [Connect external agent](https://motion.dev/docs/studio#connect-an-agent) to set one up.

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/studio-ultramotion

# Ultramotion

Ultramotion is Motion Studio's animation editing agent. Describe an edit in plain language and it changes the preview in seconds.

Ultramotion is Motion Studio's animation editing agent.

It can edit animations based on natural language up to 15x faster than even a cheap and fast coding agent. Currently, it's built on [Jev](https://typesafe.ai/), falling back to Luna for more complicated edits.

Prompt it with terms like "increase duration" or "stagger these by 0.2s" and it'll perform those edits on the same preview that you're working on. You can undo its changes the same way you'd undo your own.

Selected tracks will appear in the chat as context, or you can refer to them by name.

## Instant edits

Some edits need no language model at all. A quick suggestion that the panel applies itself, or an edit that Jev plans without falling back to Luna, shows **Instant** in the chat instead of the agent's name. [Adapt for reduced motion](https://motion.dev/docs/studio-reduced-motion#adapt-for-reduced-motion) is usually one of these.

## Allowance

Ultramotion needs Motion+ Pro. Every signed-in account also gets 5 free Ultramotion prompts each month. When the allowance is used up, the chat asks you to [connect a coding agent](https://motion.dev/docs/studio#connect-an-agent) until it resets.

## Limitations

Ultramotion achieves its speed by being narrow in scope. It only performs edits on the current animation. It isn't chatty, although it can ask for clarification.

Because of this, prompts like "add the header to this animation" won't (currently) work. This action must be performed manually via the "Add element" button.


---

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/studio-reduced-motion

# Reduced motion

Preview your site as users who ask for reduced motion see it, and edit a separate reduced motion version of each animation.

Some people set their system to ask for reduced motion. Large movement, zooms and parallax can make them feel dizzy or sick.

Motion Studio gives each animation a **reduced motion version**: what those users see instead. You can preview it, edit it on the timeline, or let Studio adapt it for you in one step.

## Reduced motion mode

Press the reduced motion button in the header to turn on **reduced motion mode**.

While it's on:

- The page plays as if your system asks for reduced motion. `window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` and `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` CSS rules both follow the button.
- The timeline shows **Editing reduced motion**. Every edit, from you or from chat, changes only the reduced motion version. The full motion animation stays as it is.
- The selected animation replays, so you can see the reduced motion version straight away.

Press the button again, or close the **Editing reduced motion** indicator, to go back to full motion.

When your system already asks for reduced motion, Studio starts in reduced motion mode and tells you so. Studio remembers the mode for the rest of the browser session. When the panel is closed, the page follows your real system setting again.

Studio's own panel animations always follow your real system setting, not the mode.

## Edit a reduced motion version

In reduced motion mode, edit the timeline as normal. Drag tracks, change keyframes and easing, or prompt Ultramotion. The first edit creates the reduced motion version. Until then, the track plays its full motion animation for everyone.

A track whose reduced motion version differs from full motion shows the reduced motion icon. Hover it to see what differs.

Right-click a track, or one of its values, for more options:

| Action | Result |
| --- | --- |
| **Remove track** | No animation for reduced motion. The element goes straight to its end state. The full motion track stays. |
| **Disable value** | One value goes straight to its end value. The other values still animate. |
| **Reset override** | Deletes the reduced motion version. Reduced motion plays the full motion animation again. |
| **Apply to base animation** | Copies the reduced motion version into the full motion animation. |
| **Delete animation** | No animation for reduced motion. The full motion animation stays. |

Reduced motion versions are available for single animations and components. Sequences and Vue components can't have one yet.

## Adapt for reduced motion

In reduced motion mode, **Adapt for reduced motion** leads the chat suggestions when the selected animation has no reduced motion version yet. Press it to give every track a reduced motion version in one step.

It follows a few simple rules:

- **Entrances** start at their end values, and only fade in. A scale up from `0.5`, or a slide in from off screen, never shows.
- **Exits** fade out where they are.
- **Other movement**, such as hover, press and state changes, runs with no motion. The element goes straight to its end values.
- **Loops** that repeat forever, or faster than three times each second, run with no motion, whatever they animate.
- Tracks that are already safe, such as an opacity or colour change, stay as they are.

Fades last between 0.15 and 0.3 seconds. Delays and staggers stay as they are.

Movement here means position, size, transforms, masks, clip paths, line drawing and other values that change an element's size or shape.

Most animations adapt instantly with no language model, so the chat shows **Instant**. When Studio can't classify a track, Ultramotion asks Luna to adapt the timeline with the same rules. Studio then checks the reply, and stops any motion the reply left in.

As with any edit, check the result in the preview, then Apply.

## Reduced motion warning

The reduced motion button turns yellow when the selected animation needs attention:

- **In full motion mode**, it moves an element on screen, or loops faster than three times each second, and has no reduced motion version.
- **In either mode**, its reduced motion version still moves, loops or flashes. For example, after you prompt "add more bounce" in reduced motion mode.

Hover the button to see why. To fix it, turn on reduced motion mode, then use **Adapt for reduced motion** or edit the animation.

The warning is advice only. Nothing is blocked. An animation that Motion already reduces, through the `reduceMotion` option or `<MotionConfig reducedMotion="user">`, doesn't show it for position, size and transforms.

## In your code

Code stays the source of truth. On Apply, Studio writes the reduced motion version as a condition on the animation's own keyframes, options or props.

In JavaScript, it checks `matchMedia`:

```js
animate(
  ".box",
  window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").matches
    ? { opacity: [0, 1] }
    : { scale: [0.5, 1], opacity: [0, 1] },
  { duration: 1 }
)
```

In React, it uses [`useReducedMotion`](https://motion.dev/docs/react-use-reduced-motion):

```jsx
export function Card() {
  const shouldReduceMotion = useReducedMotion()

  return (
    <motion.div
      animate={shouldReduceMotion ? { opacity: 1 } : { opacity: 1, y: 0 }}
    />
  )
}
```

Studio also reads conditions like these that you wrote yourself, so existing reduced motion code shows up as a reduced motion version. It reuses a `useReducedMotion()` result already in your component rather than adding a second one.

An animation guarded by the check, such as `if (shouldReduceMotion) animate(...)`, runs only for reduced motion. An animation guarded by `if (!shouldReduceMotion)` is hidden in reduced motion mode.

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install

# Install the Motion AI Kit

Install the Motion AI Kit with our easy installer script.

Install Motion AI Kit with a single command.

```
npx motion-ai
```

It prompts whether to install for this **project** or **globally**, and which agents to set up: Claude Code, Cursor, Amp, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Copilot, or a custom folder.

The installer and the `/motion` skill it installs are free and open source (MIT). The source is on GitHub at [motiondivision/ai-kit](https://github.com/motiondivision/ai-kit).

Once installed, you might need to manually activate the MCP servers via your agent's MCP options.

The installer adds the free MCP server, which searches current documentation and example metadata.

## Motion+

[Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) unlocks CSS spring generation, MotionScore audits, the transition editor and premium source. Members add the Motion+ MCP server with:

```
npx motion-ai --plus
```

Then sign in to the Motion+ MCP server from your agent's MCP settings to use these tools.

## Cursor

## Transition editor

For agents, the transition editor is automatically available anywhere that [supports MCP Apps](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/apps/overview#client-support).

It's also possible to install a real-time editor via the official Motion Extension via the [VS Code Marketplace](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=motion.motion-vscode-extension), and on the [Open VSX marketplace](https://open-vsx.org/extension/motion/motion-vscode-extension) for other editors.

## Upgrading

Re-run the installer at the same scope to upgrade:

```
npx motion-ai@latest
```

It rewrites the installed skills and the `motion` MCP configuration in place. Add `--plus` to also update the Motion+ server.

Earlier versions of the kit configured a local MCP server with a Motion+ API key. That flow is retired: if your agent still lists a `motion` server that runs an `npx` command with a `TOKEN`, remove it. The kit now uses Motion's hosted MCP servers, and Motion+ access comes from signing in via your agent instead. Your API key is no longer needed.

# Motion AI Kit: the Motion skill and MCP for your agent

Make your AI agent a Motion expert with current docs, Performance Doctor, examples search, CSS springs and a transition editor.

Motion (formerly Framer Motion) provides an official skill and MCP server for AI coding agents.

Motion AI Kit is a suite of tools that improve the quality and consistency of your AI agent's animation output.

- [**Context**](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-context)**:** Give your agent access to the latest Motion docs, your saved transitions, and the source code for 510+ premium examples.

- [**Performance Doctor**](https://motion.dev/docs/performance-doctor)**:** Find and fix animation performance issues with MotionScore's code and runtime analysis.

- [**CSS spring generation**](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-generate-css)**:** Generate `linear()` spring curves on demand. No animation library needed.

- [**Transition editor**](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-transition-editor)**:** Real-time visual editing of Motion and CSS transitions, directly in your chat or IDE.

## Features

### Context

AI is trained on data that's often out of date, or on low-quality Stack Overflow/Reddit posts.

The Motion AI Kit includes best practices, lets your agent query the latest Motion docs for knowledge of the latest APIs, as well as pulling in the source code of 510+ premium [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) examples and [Motion UI](https://motion.dev/ui) components for recommended patterns across a wide range of popular effects and interactions.

![](https://images.motion.dev/docs/jWa3w2mxUiXfBvBvynH47y5yadk.png)

[Learn more about improving AI context](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-context)

### Performance Doctor

Performance Doctor audits source code and can profile a running local site. It grades Motion, CSS, GSAP and other animations by render cost, then gives your agent specific fixes to apply.

![](https://images.motion.dev/docs/9wvYmyGqcIN79K257arf76ls6I.png)

[Learn more about Performance Doctor](https://motion.dev/docs/performance-doctor)

### Transition editing

The AI Kit enables real-time editing and preview of CSS and Motion transitions, without leaving your agent or editor. It supports editing easing curves and springs, with the latter able to generate CSS code via the `linear()` easing curve.

![](https://images.motion.dev/docs/KO5dnHOUSNGb9S73p1J7nLhoFI.gif)

Your favourite transitions can be saved and reused both in the transition editor and by your agent via the MCP.

![](https://images.motion.dev/docs/SxtfTFKwCAT1bIFEDY7JybZ30.png)

[Learn more about the transition editor](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-transition-editor)

### Generate CSS springs

The `/motion` skill can generate CSS `linear()` easing curves to create springs and other custom easing functions, using Motion without the runtime.

> For instance, a prompt like "generate a spring that's quite bouncy" will generate:

```
600ms linear(0, 0.0121 /* ... */)
```

[Learn more about CSS generation](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-generate-css)

## Install

Install once and everything above is available in every conversation. Cursor installs in one click below; Claude Code, Codex and other agents take a couple of minutes with the [install guide](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install).

Best practices and documentation search are free, with no account or configuration. [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus) unlocks CSS spring generation, MotionScore audits, the transition editor and the source code for 510+ premium examples and Motion UI components. Sign in from your editor and it takes effect on your next request.

# Docs & examples for your agent

Motion+ (paid). Best practices (the free /motion skill, https://github.com/motiondivision/ai-kit) and documentation search are free. Example and Motion UI source need Motion+, through the Motion+ MCP server. Setup: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install. Give your AI editor always-current Motion docs to search, plus source code for every premium example on Motion+.

Agents have supercharged our development workflows, but even now they still struggle to produce great animation code.

For example, have you ever fought with your agent about still importing Motion from `"framer-motion"`? This is because it's trained on out-of-date information.

Or has it produced an animation that's **almost** right but ends up requiring a lot of prompting to fix? This is because agents can't visualise timing or easing curves, so they guess at values that often feel wrong.

The [Motion AI Kit](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit) gives your agent the right information at the right time, using:

- **Motion documentation:** the full, current docs, searchable by your agent.

- **Example source code:** every premium [example](https://motion.dev/examples) from [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus).

- **Your saved transitions:** anything transition build in the [transition editor](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-transition-editor).

- **Best practises:** Handwritten animation tips by the Motion team. Free and open source.

In short, it turns your AI editor into a Motion expert.

## Documentation

This ensures your agent is working with the latest information, not missing or out-of-date training data.


## Examples

- **Zero hallucinations:** The code comes from the official Motion repository, not a generic LLM training set.

## Best practises

The AI Kit installs a `/motion` skill: handwritten animation rules straight from the Motion team. It detects your platform and any UI libraries, and applies the correct guidance.

The skill is free and open source (MIT), and needs no account. Its source is on GitHub at [motiondivision/ai-kit](https://github.com/motiondivision/ai-kit).

For instance, it will know which values the browser can animate on the GPU, like `transform`, `opacity` and `filter`, and which trigger layout or paint. Or what tone of animation to use based on the intended audience of the project.

The result is animation code that feels considered, performs well, and needs far less back and forth to get right.

# Performance Doctor

Motion+ (paid). Runs through the Motion+ MCP server. Setup: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install. Find and fix animation performance issues in source code and running local sites with Performance Doctor.

Performance Doctor finds and fixes slow animations.

It combines source analysis and local-runtime profiling to find animation performance issues before they ship. Then it opens a report card in your chat, where one click sends any finding back to your agent to fix.

```
/motion audit
```

It grades animations using the same S-to-F system as MotionScore Audit.

## Install

```
/motion audit the homepage
```

Performance Doctor is part of the **Motion AI Kit**. Installing AI Kit sets up the `/motion` skill, including its performance audit, for every supported editor. Follow the [AI Kit install guide](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install).

## Usage

Ask your agent to run a performance audit (adding `/motion` might be required in some agents).

By default, this runs a broad search of CSS, Motion, GSAP and Anime.js APIs in your codebase.

It's possible to limit the scope by passing a directory or filename:

```
/motion audit @components/Button
```

Additionally, you can scope the audit to specific types of animations, or specific APIs:

```
Can you perform a /motion audit of all scroll animations
```

Ask for fixes in the same prompt and the agent fixes what it finds without asking first:

```
/motion fix animation performance on the homepage
```

### Runtime audits

Mention "runtime", a specific page, or pass a URL to profile the page as it runs:

```
/motion performance audit on http://localhost:3000
```

Your agent runs `npx motionscore <url> --agent`, which loads the page in a local browser and records every animation it plays. Static and runtime results go into the same report. A runtime finding points at the element's selector, with a hint for where to look in your code.

## The report card

When the audit finishes, your agent opens the report card in the chat.

### Rank

The card leads with an overall rank, from S to F, across every animation the audit found. Beside it, a headline counts the animations that need work, and the rank you'll reach once they're fixed.

Under that, a bar shows how many animations sit at each grade. The idea is for it to be top-heavy: more S, A and B than below.

### Findings

Every animation with a practical upgrade gets a finding, worst first. Each finding shows:

- **Grade:** Its current grade and the grade the fix reaches, for instance F â A.

- **What:** What the animation does, with its filename and line number.

- **Why:** Why it's slow, in terms of the render pipeline.

- **Fix:** How to make it faster.

- **Example fix:** A short diff of the change.

Animations that already pass are listed, collapsed, at the bottom of the card.

### Fixing

Click **Fix** on a finding to send it to your agent, in the same chat that ran the audit. It already knows the finding, so there's nothing to explain.

The agent makes the edit, then checks it: it runs your project's type check, lint and tests for the changed files, and grades the animation again. While it works, the card shows the file it's fixing.

If the check passes, the finding changes to **Fixed**, with its new grade, and the rank climbs. Changed your mind? Click **Undo** and the agent restores the original code.

If the check fails, or the fix would change how the animation looks, the agent rolls back its edit and the card says why. Your code is unchanged, and **Try again** sends it back.

**Fix all** sends every open finding. The agent works through them one at a time, in order.

**Copy prompt** copies a standalone prompt for a finding, so you can paste it into a different agent or chat.

**Warning:**
  MCP Apps are currently hidden by default in Cursor. The report appears
  collapsed as "Explored 1 tool", with the Motion icon. Click it to open the
  card.

## Terminals

The report card is an [MCP App](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/apps/overview#client-support). Hosts that can't show MCP Apps, for instance Claude Code CLI and Codex CLI, get the same report as a numbered list instead:

```
Performance Doctor report dr_3f9a1c0e2b7d Â· acme-storefront Â· src, static
Rank B. 4 of 18 animations need work. Fixing them reaches rank A.

1. F â A  Layout thrashing Â· components/Parallax.tsx:18
   What: Each card reads its position, then writes a transform, inside one scroll handler.
   Why: Every read after a write forces a synchronous layout, once per card, every frame.
   Fix: Read every position first with frame.read, then write the transforms with frame.render.
2. D â S  Animating width Â· components/Drawer.tsx:34
   ...

Passing: 14 animations (9 S, 3 A, 2 B).
```

Reply with the findings you want fixed:

```
fix 1 and 3
```

Or simply "fix all". The agent fixes and checks each one in turn, just as it does from the card.

## Tiers

The broad tier list used for grading animations is as follows:

| Tier  | Cost                         | Animated properties                                                                                         |
| ----- | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **S** | Compositor only              | `transform`, `opacity`, `filter`, `clip-path`, `ScrollTimeline`                                             |
| **A** | JS â Compositor              | Same properties, but set from JavaScript each frame                                                         |
| **B** | One-time layout read â S/A   | `layout`, `layoutId`                                                                                        |
| **C** | Repaint each frame           | `background-color`, `color`, `border-radius`, `box-shadow`, CSS variables, SVG attributes, View Transitions |
| **D** | Layout + repaint each frame  | `width`, `height`, `margin`, `padding`, `top`/`left`, `font-size`, `gap`                                    |
| **F** | Forced sync layout per cycle | Interleaved DOM reads/writes, `:root` CSS variable animation                                                |

The full explanation of these grades can be found in the [MotionScore methodology](https://score.motion.dev/methodology). For deeper editorial background, read the [Web Animation Performance Tier List](https://motion.dev/magazine/web-animation-performance-tier-list).


---

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-generate-css

# Generate CSS

Motion+ (paid). A tool on the Motion+ MCP server. Setup: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install. Generate CSS spring and bounce easing curves directly from your LLM via the Motion AI Kit MCP.

With [Motion+](https://motion.dev/plus), the Motion AI Kit can generate Motion springs and other easing functions as a CSS [`linear()` easing function](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/easing-function/linear), no animation library required.

For example, you can ask your LLM to "Make a CSS spring curve, snappy but bouncy" and it will generate a `linear()` curve with the right duration and resolution to ensure your animation is smooth:

```
// LLM receives:
500ms linear(0, 0.009, 0.035 2.1%, /* ... */)
```

`linear()` can create all kinds of easing curves that were previously impossible with CSS, like spring and bounce effects.

The downside to `linear()` is its maintainability. With Motion, it's usually easy to write and understand this kind of animation:

```
{ type: "spring", bounce: 0.2 }
```

However, with CSS and `linear()`, you need to visit a generation tool to output a property that looks like this:

```
linear(
  0, 0.009, 0.035 2.1%, 0.141, 0.281 6.7%, 0.723 12.9%, 0.938 16.7%, 1.017,
  1.077, 1.121, 1.149 24.3%, 1.159, 1.163, 1.161, 1.154 29.9%, 1.129 32.8%,
  1.051 39.6%, 1.017 43.1%, 0.991, 0.977 51%, 0.974 53.8%, 0.975 57.1%,
  0.997 69.8%, 1.003 76.9%, 1.004 83.8%, 1
)
```

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-generate-css


---

Source: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-transition-editor

# Transition editor

Motion+ (paid). A tool on the Motion+ MCP server. Setup: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install. Edit your CSS and Motion animations visually with the Motion AI Kit.

Edit CSS and Motion transition using visual controls with [Motion AI Kit](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install).

The transition editor is available for any agent that [supports MCP Apps](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/apps/overview#client-support). There's additionally a real-time [transition editor](https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-install#transition-editor), available for VS Code and code editors that support Open VSX.

{/* Inline width beats the fresh layout's full-bleed rule for direct-child
    article images â this one is a phone-narrow app and reads best small. */}
<img
  src="https://images.motion.dev/docs/transition-mcp-app.png"
  alt="The Motion transition editor MCP App"
  width="250"
  style={{ width: 250 }}
/>

- **CSS & Motion:** Animate with either (or both!)
- **Real-time updates:** The VS Code extension edits your code in real-time. The MCP App can apply changes via your agent.
- **Cloud saves:** Like a transition? Save it to reuse later.
- **Share transitions:** Saved transitions appear on your Motion+ profile for others to download.

![](https://images.motion.dev/docs/KO5dnHOUSNGb9S73p1J7nLhoFI.gif)

## MCP App

Your agent may choose to open the transition editor whenever editing a transition, or you can manually open with:

```
/motion open the transition editor
```

**Warning:**
  There is currently a bug in Cursor where MCP Apps are hidden. Click the most
  recent "thinking" section to reveal.

Once you're happy with the transition, click Apply to implement with your agent.

## VSCode Extension

Open the Motion panel and highlight any Motion or CSS animation or transition to open the edit controls.

![Screenshot of the AI Kit Extension editing animations](https://images.motion.dev/docs/5Rkh6xZJZQkbXEIn5t7YffuyKqg.png)

Every change updates your code instantly via AST-powered edits, so there's no copy-pasting or manual syncing.

Edits made to your code will preserve most formatting and respect overrides like `transition-delay`.

The rest of this page is for Motion+ members: https://motion.dev/docs/ai-kit-transition-editor
